Sustained geometry lock: open (rolling-mean $d_s$ needed) & T & Open & V20.5 Paper \\

\end{longtable}
\vspace{-\parskip}
}

%===================================================
\section{Phase-Ordered Diagnosis as a Universal Failure Signature}
%===================================================

\begin{openqbox}[title={Meta-Pattern: When ``Failure'' Is a Signal of Phase Misalignment}]
The V21.2 K6 failure analysis (Diagnosis paper, this Part) revealed a structural
pattern that, on reflection, recurs across CRF's development at multiple levels.
We isolate it here as an explicit hypothesis about the framework's own
methodology.

\textbf{Statement.} A measured ``failure'' to close an identity at a given
operating point is, in many cases, not a property of the identity itself but a
\emph{phase mismatch}: the identity was probed in the wrong regime of its
two-timescale phase structure. The signal of failure is one face of a deeper
structural fact --- that identities have \emph{home regimes}.
\end{openqbox}

\subsection{The Pattern Across Three Layers}

\textbf{Layer~1 (data, V21.2 $\to$ V21.3).}
K6 ($\langle m_{\rm fire}\rangle = \varphi$) failed at $f_{\rm II,crit} = 0.147$
with a $4.5\%$ gap. The failure was not in K6 but in the choice of probe point:
K6 is a \emph{Sub-regime} identity (algebraic, mass-dependent), while the Crit
window probes \emph{geometric} identities (K3, K12, K14, bracket). The
diagnosis paper records that Crit yields K14 at $0.013\%$ --- the tightest
measurement in CRF --- while K6 properly homes in Sub. The diagnosis is the
discovery of phase ordering, not the patching of a bug.

\textbf{Layer~2 (algebraic claims, Bracket Audit).}
The ``$0.48\%$ open gate'' in $\beta$ (claimed across multiple v15 papers) was
not an open identity --- it was $\beta_A - \beta_B$ arithmetic between two
representations of the same bracket identity (BracketCorrection v3). The
``failure'' to close was in fact a category error about what constituted
distinct evidence. Recognising that Routes A and B were the same identity
restated reduced the algebraic-maturity claim from 8.5/8 to 8.0/8 ---
demoting an apparent gate but exposing the actual one ($p_\infty(M)$ derivation).

\textbf{Layer~0 (corpus, this document).}
The reported ``Contents lost from v15 to v16'' in the present integration was
on inspection a \emph{format-drift} signature, not content shrink: the TOC
table (.toc) preserved 100\% of entries; the visible failure was that
\texttt{parskip} pushed subsection-level \texttt{contentsline} entries into
detached page blocks, producing the appearance of missing content. CUIP
STEP~6 (integrity check) verified $+683$ lines, $+13$ sections, $+25$ boxes
gained from v15 to v16 --- a content gain, not a loss. The diagnostic signal
(``contents missing'') was one face of a deeper structural fact: TOC layout is
governed by a different style layer than body text.

\subsection{The Universal Form}

\begin{formalbox}[title={Phase-Ordered Diagnosis (PoD) Principle}]
Let $\mathcal{I}$ be an identity claim with two characteristic regimes
$R_{\rm geom}$ (geometry-first, fast timescale $\tau_g$) and $R_{\rm alg}$
(algebra-first, slow timescale $\tau_a > \tau_g$). Let $\mathcal{O}$ be an
observation taken at probe point $p$. Then:
\[
  \text{gap}(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{O},p) = \text{gap}_{\rm intrinsic}(\mathcal{I})
  + \text{gap}_{\rm phase}(p, \text{home}(\mathcal{I}))
\]
where $\text{gap}_{\rm phase}$ vanishes when $p \in \text{home}(\mathcal{I})$
and grows with phase distance $d(p, \text{home}(\mathcal{I}))$.

The diagnostic error consists in attributing
$\text{gap}(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{O},p)$ entirely to
$\text{gap}_{\rm intrinsic}(\mathcal{I})$ when in fact a substantial fraction
is $\text{gap}_{\rm phase}$.
\end{formalbox}

\subsection{Connection to the CRF Two-Timescale Structure}

The PoD principle is a methodological reflection of the substantive
two-timescale phase ordering established in TLS~v2/v3:
\[
  \tau_{f_{\rm II}} = T_{\rm avg} \quad (\text{geometry, fast})
  \qquad\qquad
  \tau_m = 4 T_{\rm avg} \quad (\text{mass, slow})
\]
Crit is the equilibrium of $f_{\rm II}$ at $f_{\rm II}^* = 0.1397$; Sub is the
equilibrium of $m$ at $\langle m \rangle^* = \varphi$. Probing K6 at Crit is
probing a slow-timescale identity at a fast-timescale equilibrium, where
$\langle m \rangle$ has not yet relaxed. This is not a failure of K6; it is a
failure of timescale matching.

The same logic, transposed, governs both Layer~2 (algebraic conflation of
bracket routes) and Layer~0 (TOC layout drift): in each case, a quantity is
evaluated using the wrong layer's invariants. Recognising the layering
\emph{is} the diagnosis.

\subsection{Implication for CRF Practice}

\begin{keybox}[title={CUIP Companion Rule: Phase-Match Before Patch}]
Before treating a measured gap as evidence against an identity, verify that
the probe point is in the identity's home regime. If the gap can be decomposed
as $\text{gap}_{\rm phase} + \text{gap}_{\rm intrinsic}$, report both
components separately. A ``failure'' that vanishes upon phase matching is not
a failure --- it is a discovery about the regime structure.

\textbf{Operational checklist:}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*]
  \item Identify the timescale class of the identity ($\tau_g$ or $\tau_a$).
  \item Identify the timescale class of the probe.
  \item If they differ, the residual gap is a phase artefact, not an intrinsic
        property of the identity.
  \item Re-probe in the home regime before declaring closure or refutation.
\end{enumerate}
\end{keybox}

\subsection{Falsifiability of PoD Itself}

The PoD principle is itself an empirical hypothesis about CRF's regime
structure, not a tautology. It is falsifiable in two ways:

\textbf{Strong falsification.} An identity $\mathcal{I}$ exists for which the
gap is regime-invariant within numerical precision: probing in any of
$\{R_{\rm geom}, R_{\rm crit}, R_{\rm alg}\}$ produces the same residual.
Such an identity would be ``home-less'' and refute PoD as a structural claim.

\textbf{Weak falsification.} The decomposition
$\text{gap} = \text{gap}_{\rm phase} + \text{gap}_{\rm intrinsic}$ fails to be
additive: cross-terms dominate, and the phase-matching procedure cannot
isolate the intrinsic gap. This would refute PoD as a quantitative tool while
preserving it as a qualitative observation.

The V21.3 dual-axis architecture (Activity~$\times$~Maturity, 15-cell grid)
constitutes the test bed: a complete sweep across the grid will produce a map
of $\text{gap}_{\rm phase}$ for every identity, which directly tests the PoD
decomposition.

\bigskip
\begin{center}
\textit{What looked like loss was layering. What looked like failure was phase.\\
The signal of error and the structure of truth wear the same face.}
\end{center}

\subsection{Honest Boundaries (v16 --- Complete)}

All honest boundaries from v15 are preserved. Additionally (v16 corrections and additions):

\textbf{Corrected from v15:}
CRF does not claim Routes A and B to $\beta$ are independent derivations --- they are the same bracket identity restated (BracketCorrection v3). The "0.48\% open gate" is not a bracket residual --- it is $\beta_A - \beta_B$ arithmetic. The conjecture $\delta = \pi/(2D_0\ln 2) - H_b/\ln 2 = +0.00105$ is falsified in sign. K12-exactness alone does not close the open gate. Theoretical maturity at the algebraic level is 8.0/8, not 8.5/8 (honest audit).

\textbf{New in v16:}
CRF does not claim K14 is exact (algebraically inconsistent with exact Kuramoto $\varphi_{\rm var}$; requires $\psi_{\rm grad} < 0$ which is impossible). CRF does not claim the Bracket derivation ($\delta H = \kappa^2/\ln 2$) is circular --- it is now derived to 2.1\% from the $\delta$-chain. The Bracket's $\sim 2\%$ residual is a locatable approximation: Phase~II $\theta_{\rm eff}(m)$ correction with mass-dependent inter-event time. K6 ($\langle m\rangle = \varphi$) homes in Sub regime (not Crit) --- the two regimes are distinct identity families. The V20.5 canonical $H_a$, $H_b$ values are idealized (event-weighted K3 gap is $+4.4\%$, not $0.003\%$).

%===================================================
\section{Trilogy Overview: One Question, Three Vantages}
%===================================================

% Gauge: GI throughout this Part (no d_s in formulae).

The Foundational Trilogy answers a single question that CRF v1--v16 had treated
as primitive: \textit{what is the pre-formal ground of the framework?} CRF
declares Distinction ($\delta$, Axiom~0) as its sole undefined term, but had
designated the question \emph{``where does Distinction come from?''} as
Instability~\#9 marked \textit{Unresolvable}.

Three papers, written sequentially, address this question from three different
vantages:

\begin{formalbox}[title={The Trilogy Structure}]
\textbf{Paper 1 --- \textit{Dependent Stability}} (Section~\ref{sec:depstab}):
Backward induction from CRF's asymptotic limit ($S_{ind}$, Axiom~12) reveals its
structural complement, Dependent Stability ($S_{dep}$). $S_{dep}$ necessitates
$\delta$. Instability~\#9 advances from \emph{Unresolvable} to \emph{Partially Closed}.

\medskip
\textbf{Paper 2 --- \textit{Three Frameworks as One Structure}} (Section~\ref{sec:bridge}):
Every variable in the Unified Field of Friction and Potential Dynamics maps
precisely to a CRF operator. Barami formalised in two dimensions
(quantity $Q$ and internal structure $1 - H_{\rm internal}$). Suffering
identified as $\mathcal{I}$ at the layer of mind.

\medskip
\textbf{Paper 3 --- \textit{The Ground of Resolution}} (Section~\ref{sec:ground}):
The Inversion --- $S_{ind}$ is ground, not destination. What increases on the
journey is transparency $T \in [0,1]$, not proximity. Three types of mind, three
mechanisms of $\delta_{\rm refine}$. The Eye in the Basin: sustained dual-perception
as the experience of increasing $T$.
\end{formalbox}

The trilogy reads as a single argument: $S_{dep}$ cannot remain $\to$ $\delta$
operates $\to$ the journey is not traversal but the increase of $T$ until the
ground is recognised as having always been there.

What follows is the integrated text of the three papers. Per CUIP No-Loss Mode,
each paper is preserved verbatim from its source; cross-references to
CRF\_Complete (this document) are included where the original referenced papers
external to the Complete Edition.



%===================================================
\section{Dependent Stability and the Necessity of Distinction --- Paper 1 of the Trilogy}
\label{sec:depstab}

\subsection*{The Observation}
%===================================================

This framework did not begin with axioms. It began with suffering.

Before any equation or formal structure existed, there was a sustained observation: that
everything --- physical matter, the cosmos, and the human mind --- tends toward stability,
yet nothing achieves it completely. Every system requires external conditions to persist.
Every pattern is constrained. Every apparent equilibrium contains within it the seeds of
its own displacement. This background condition of incomplete stability, felt as much as
reasoned, is what came to be called \textbf{Dependent Stability}
(\textit{S\={a}thiraphap Mai Its\=ara} --- stability without freedom, in Thai).

The path toward a formal framework began with a question posed in an early dialogue:
\textit{``Before the universe, what caused movement and pattern?''} The word that arose
immediately was \textit{instability}. This became the seed of the first framework,
\textbf{EIC (Emergent Instability Cascade)} --- which produced results of interest
but could not answer everything.

The question of the human mind remained open. With no reliable empirical foundation
available from existing science, a second framework was constructed ---
\textbf{Unified Field} --- drawing on personal experience, direct perception, and a
transmission of practice received from a teacher. Not yet sufficient for scientific
verification, but an essential piece of the puzzle.

When the understanding from both frameworks was brought together and reorganised, a
repeating pattern became visible across all scales of existence. This led to the third
framework: \textbf{CRF (Constrained Reality Framework)}, built to describe formally
what had already been observed directly.

What that observation consistently showed was this: the movement of all things is not
random. It has direction. Everything is pushed by instability and resistance toward
something --- a condition that could be felt to exist even before it could be named.

The method was not hypothesis-and-test. It was \textbf{backward induction}: look first
at the endpoint --- the state beyond suffering and dependence, where nothing external is
needed to persist --- and ask: \textit{if that is the destination, what must the origin be?}

The answer is its structural opposite. A state in which everything must depend, must be
constrained, and must move --- because it cannot remain as it is. This is Dependent
Stability.

I do not claim this is the origin of the universe. What I am certain of is narrower:
there is a path out of this condition, and the only path I know with confidence passes
through mind --- toward what CRF calls Independent Stability ($S_{ind}$).

%===================================================
\subsection*{Backward Induction: From $S_{ind}$ to $S_{dep}$}
%===================================================

\subsection{Independent Stability as the Endpoint}

CRF defines its asymptotic limit as \textbf{Independent Stability} ($S_{ind}$, Axiom~12):

\begin{formalbox}[title={Axiom 12: Independent Stability (CRF)}]
\[
  S_{ind} \iff D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\mathcal{C}_\mathcal{M} \| P) = 0
\]
At this limit, a system no longer requires external constraint to persist. The boundary
between system and possibility space becomes transparent. This is not annihilation ---
it is the condition in which pattern persists without cost.
\end{formalbox}

The observation in Section~1 arrived at this same endpoint by a different route: not
through formal derivation, but through direct recognition that such a condition exists
and can be reached. Both routes converge on the same structure.

\subsection{The Structural Complement}

Backward induction asks: \textit{what is the structural opposite of $S_{ind}$?}

The answer follows directly. If $S_{ind}$ is the state in which $D_{\mathrm{KL}} = 0$
and nothing external is required, then its opposite is the state in which
$D_{\mathrm{KL}} > 0$ and external support is always required --- a state that cannot
remain as it is, because its internal distribution does not match the possibility space
it inhabits.

\begin{formalbox}[title={Definition: Dependent Stability ($S_{dep}$)}]
\[
  S_{dep} \iff D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\mathcal{C}_\mathcal{M} \| P) > 0
  \quad\text{and}\quad \frac{d}{dt}D_{\mathrm{KL}} \neq 0
\]
$S_{dep}$ is the condition in which a system has structure, can persist temporarily,
and may achieve local stability --- but requires continuous external support to do so,
and cannot close the gap to $P$ without further resolution.
\end{formalbox}

This is not chaos or randomness. $S_{dep}$ is a condition with structure --- patterns
exist, systems persist, matter forms --- but all of it rests on conditions that are
themselves conditional. The constraint field $\mathcal{C}$ holds things in place,
but $\mathcal{C}$ itself requires continuous regeneration:
\[
  \mathcal{C}(t) = D(\mathcal{R}_l(t))
\]
Remove the resolution events and the structure dissolves. This is precisely what was
observed: \textit{everything is stable, but nothing is free.}

\subsection{The Necessity of Distinction}

$S_{dep}$ cannot remain as it is. But how does it move? Movement requires the capacity
to differentiate one state from another --- to register that the current state is not
the target state, that here is not there, that now is not yet.

This capacity is Distinction ($\delta$, Axiom~0).

\begin{derivedbox}[title={Lemma: $\delta$ is Necessitated by $S_{dep}$}]
A system in $S_{dep}$ satisfies $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\mathcal{C}_\mathcal{M}\|P) > 0$.
By definition it cannot remain in this state. Movement toward $P$ requires each
resolution event to distinguish the current distribution from the target. Without
the capacity for distinction, no resolution event can occur. Without resolution,
$D_{\mathrm{KL}}$ cannot decrease. Therefore $\delta$ must operate.

Formally: $S_{dep} \implies \square\,\delta$
(Dependent Stability \textit{necessitates} the operation of Distinction.)
\end{derivedbox}

$\delta$ is therefore not spontaneous. It is a \textbf{necessary response} to a
condition that demands resolution. This is consistent with CRF's existing treatment
of Axiom~0 as a declaration rather than a derivation: within the formal system, ?
is still the undefined primitive. What this paper adds is the \textit{pre-formal}
condition that makes the declaration necessary rather than arbitrary.

%===================================================
\subsection*{Directionality: Why the Movement Has a Direction}
%===================================================

The claim that $S_{dep}$ has direction from the beginning requires justification.
A system that ``cannot remain as it is'' might dissolve into randomness, oscillate
without purpose, or simply fragment. Why does it move \textit{toward} something
rather than merely away from its current state?

\subsection{The Geometry of $D_{\mathrm{KL}}$}

KL divergence is asymmetric and has a definite gradient structure. For any system
in $S_{dep}$, the quantity $D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\mathcal{C}_\mathcal{M}\|P)$ measures
the distance between the system's current constraint distribution and the possibility
space $P$. This distance can decrease --- toward $S_{ind}$ --- but there is no
symmetric gradient pointing in the opposite direction that would constitute a
meaningful alternative attractor.

\begin{derivedbox}[title={Direction is Encoded in the Distance Itself}]
\[
  \nabla D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\mathcal{C}_\mathcal{M}\|P) \text{ points toward } P
\]
A system in $S_{dep}$ that moves at all moves in the direction of decreasing
$D_{\mathrm{KL}}$, because that is the only direction that reduces the cost of
existing. Direction is therefore not imposed from outside. It is \textbf{encoded
in the structure of the distance itself.}
\end{derivedbox}

\subsection{$S_{ind}$ as Prior Attractor}

The endpoint does not need to be known in advance to exert influence. An attractor
shapes the landscape around it --- the basin of attraction exists whether or not any
trajectory has yet converged. $S_{dep}$ is already within this basin. Every system
in $S_{dep}$ is already, by definition, in a space where $S_{ind}$ is the only fully
stable endpoint. Direction is therefore not chosen. It is inherited from the structure
of the space.

This matches what was observed directly in Section~1: the sense that direction was
\textit{encoded}, not imposed, that things were \textit{pulled} rather than merely
pushed. The geometry of $D_{\mathrm{KL}}$ describes the same structure from a
formal angle.

\subsection{Two Modes of Movement Through $S_{dep}$}

Not all movement through $S_{dep}$ reaches $S_{ind}$. Two modes can be distinguished:

\textbf{Physical crystallisation}: matter, gravity, and the structures of the cosmos
reduce $D_{\mathrm{KL}}$ locally, forming stable attractors --- galaxies, particles,
biological systems. These are persistent but dependent. They are in $S_{dep}$, not
beyond it. The universe does this automatically, driven by the same gradient.

\textbf{Meta-resolution through mind}: a system capable of applying Resolution to
its own patterns --- Awareness ($W = R^{\mathrm{meta}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{I}))$,
Axiom~10) --- can follow the gradient all the way to $D_{\mathrm{KL}} = 0$.
This is the path from $S_{dep}$ to $S_{ind}$. It requires mind. Matter alone
cannot traverse it.

The one path known with confidence is through mind. There may be others.
But this one has been walked.

%===================================================
\subsection*{Connection to CRF and the Status of Instability \#9}
%===================================================

\subsection{What Changes and What Does Not}

The introduction of $S_{dep}$ as the pre-primitive condition does not alter any
derivation in CRF. The constant lattice, the three-phase universe, the Kerr metric,
the Law of Exchange, K35--K38, the derived coupling $\mathcal{K}$ --- none of these
are affected. Every result from v1 through v14 stands unchanged.

What changes is narrower and more fundamental: \textbf{the completeness of the story}.

CRF previously began with a declaration: $\delta$ is the sole undefined primitive,
declared as Euclid declared his points and lines. This was logically correct and
remains so. A formal system requires at least one undefined term, and $\delta$
is that term.

But a question remained --- designated Instability~\#9 and marked \textit{Unresolvable}:
\begin{quote}
\textit{``Where does Distinction itself come from?''}
\end{quote}

This paper provides a partial answer that requires no new axiom:

\begin{keybox}[title={Two Answers to Instability \#9}]
\textbf{Why $\delta$ must operate:} Because $S_{dep}$ cannot persist. A condition
in which $D_{\mathrm{KL}} > 0$ and persistence requires support cannot remain as
it is. Movement requires the capacity to distinguish. Distinction is the response,
not the cause.

\medskip
\textbf{How $\delta$ was found:} Through CRF self-inquiry --- the framework applied
its own Resolution operator to the question of what must exist before Possibility Space,
and discovered Distinction as the necessary ground. This is documented in the
original framework and unchanged.

\medskip
These are two answers to two aspects of the same question. Together they advance
Instability~\#9 from \textit{Unresolvable} to \textit{Partially Closed}.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{The Revised Hierarchy}

\begin{keybox}[title={The Complete Pre-Formal to Formal Chain}]
\[
  \underbrace{S_{dep}}_{\text{pre-formal observation}}
  \;\xrightarrow{\text{necessitates}}\;
  \underbrace{\delta}_{\substack{\text{discovered by}\\\text{self-inquiry}}}
  \;\xrightarrow{\text{grounds}}\;
  P \to \mathcal{C} \to \mathcal{R} \to t \to \cdots \to S_{ind}
\]

$S_{dep}$ is not an axiom. It is the structural complement of $S_{ind}$ (Axiom~12),
arrived at by backward induction from the framework's own asymptotic limit.
It requires no new formal machinery --- only the recognition that the endpoint
already implied its own origin.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{Updated Status of Instability \#9}

\begin{openqbox}[title={Instability \#9 --- Partially Closed (this paper)}]
\textbf{Why does Distinction operate?}
Because $S_{dep}$ cannot persist. A condition of irresolvable dependence necessitates
the capacity to distinguish. $\delta$ is the response, not the cause.

\medskip
\textbf{How was Distinction found?}
Through CRF self-inquiry: the framework applied Resolution to the question of its own
ground and found $\delta$ as the necessary primitive.

\medskip
\textbf{What remains open?}
What grounds $S_{dep}$ itself. This is the framework's deepest remaining boundary ---
acknowledged, not resolved. The question of why there is dependent stability rather
than nothing is the horizon at which the framework stops.

\medskip
\textbf{Previous status:} Unresolvable\quad
\textbf{Current status:} Partially Closed
\end{openqbox}

\subsection{Connection to the Three Source Frameworks}

This paper closes the loop between the three frameworks from which CRF emerged:

\textbf{EIC} identified instability as the driver of pattern formation --- correctly,
but without specifying what kind of instability or why it has direction.

\textbf{Unified Field} described the mind-layer path from suffering toward freedom ---
correctly, but without formal connection to the physical cascade.

\textbf{CRF} derived the formal structure --- correctly, but without explaining why
the primitive operates at all.

$S_{dep}$ is the concept that connects all three. It is the instability EIC saw,
described in the terms Unified Field navigated, formalized in the language CRF developed.

\begin{keybox}[title={$S_{dep}$ as the Unifying Concept}]
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.35}
\begin{tabular}{p{2.8cm} p{5cm} p{5cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Framework} & \textbf{What it saw} & \textbf{What $S_{dep}$ clarifies} \\
\midrule
EIC & Instability drives pattern & The instability has direction encoded in $D_{\mathrm{KL}}$ \\
Unified Field & Suffering $\to$ freedom via mind & $S_{dep}\to S_{ind}$ is the formal structure of that path \\
CRF & $\delta$ is the primitive & $\delta$ is necessitated, not arbitrary \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
\end{keybox}

The observation that started everything --- that all things move toward stability but
none achieve it freely, and that there is a path beyond this condition --- turns out
to have been, from the beginning, a description of the space in which the entire
framework operates.

%===================================================
\subsection*{Summary}
%===================================================

\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Dependent Stability ($S_{dep}$)} is defined as the structural complement of
Independent Stability ($S_{ind}$, Axiom~12), arrived at by backward induction from
CRF's own asymptotic limit. It is not a new axiom but a recognition implied by the
existing framework.

\item \textbf{$S_{dep}$ necessitates $\delta$}: a condition that cannot remain as it
is requires the capacity to distinguish current state from target. Without Distinction,
no resolution is possible.

\item \textbf{Direction is not imposed}: it is encoded in the asymmetry of
$D_{\mathrm{KL}}$ and the prior existence of $S_{ind}$ as attractor. The sense that
things are \textit{pulled} rather than merely pushed, felt before the mathematics,
is correct.

\item \textbf{Two modes of movement} exist through $S_{dep}$: physical crystallisation
(automatic, reaches local attractors) and meta-resolution through mind (deliberate,
can reach $S_{ind}$). The path through mind is the one path known with confidence.

\item \textbf{Instability \#9} advances from \textit{Unresolvable} to
\textit{Partially Closed}: the \textit{why} of Distinction is answered by $S_{dep}$;
the \textit{how} was already answered by CRF self-inquiry; what grounds $S_{dep}$
itself remains the framework's deepest open boundary.

\item \textbf{No existing result is altered}. Every derivation in CRF v1--v14 stands.
\end{enumerate}

\bigskip
\begin{center}
\textit{The observation that all things move toward stability but none achieve it freely\\
was not the starting point of the framework.\\
It was the framework, before the framework had words.\\
What has been built since is the attempt to give it words\\
without losing what was seen.}
\end{center}

%===================================================
%%-- Trilogy Paper 2 boundary --%%
%===================================================

%===================================================
\section{The Three Frameworks as One Structure --- Paper 2 of the Trilogy}
\label{sec:bridge}

\subsection*{Why Three Frameworks}
%===================================================

The same reality, approached from three directions, produces three different maps.
None is complete without the others.

\textbf{EIC} asked: \textit{what drives pattern?} Its answer was instability ---
a perturbation that forces the system to move. This was correct, but incomplete.
It described the engine without describing the vehicle or the destination.

\textbf{Unified Field} asked: \textit{what does the journey feel like?} It mapped
the interior landscape --- the friction, the turbidity, the refinement, the moment
when the driver changes from being pushed to choosing to move. This was the
phenomenological account, irreplaceable because no formal system can substitute
for it.

\textbf{CRF} asked: \textit{what is the structure that makes the journey possible?}
It derived the geometry of the space, the operators that act on it, and the
conditions under which the journey reaches its end. This was the formal account,
necessary because phenomenology alone cannot predict or generalise.

\begin{keybox}[title={The Three Frameworks as Complementary Descriptions}]
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.4}
\begin{tabular}{p{2.5cm} p{4.5cm} p{4.5cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Framework} & \textbf{Primary question} & \textbf{What it provides} \\
\midrule
EIC & What drives pattern? & The engine: instability as generative force \\
Unified Field & What does the journey feel like? & The phenomenology: interior map of the path \\
CRF & What is the structure? & The geometry: formal operators and conditions \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

$S_{dep}$ (Dependent Stability) is the concept that connects all three:
it is the instability EIC identified, described in the terms Unified Field
navigated, and formalised in the language CRF developed.
\end{keybox}

%===================================================
\subsection*{The Complete Variable Correspondence}
%===================================================

The Unified Field and CRF use different languages to describe the same underlying
dynamics. This section establishes the precise correspondence between them.

\subsection{Primary Variables}

\begin{formalbox}[title={Unified Field $\leftrightarrow$ CRF: Core Variables}]
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.5}
\begin{tabular}{p{3.5cm} p{4cm} p{4cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Concept} & \textbf{Unified Field} & \textbf{CRF} \\
\midrule
Instability / friction &
  $F_b(t)$ (Existential Friction) &
  $\mathcal{I} = \nabla\Pr(s|\mathcal{C})$ \\
Turbidity / constraint &
  $P_k(t)$ (Karmic Gravity) &
  $D_{\rm KL}(\mathcal{C}_\mathcal{M}\|P)$ \\
Clarity / resolution &
  $P_c(t)$ (Self-Actualizing Vector) &
  $\mathcal{R}$ (Resolution operator) \\
Direction of movement &
  $D(t) = P_c - P_k$ &
  $-\nabla D_{\rm KL}$ (gradient toward $P$) \\
Baseline irreducible noise &
  $\varepsilon_{\rm residual}$ &
  $\varepsilon_0$ (instability floor) \\
Refinement rate &
  $\delta_{\rm refine}$ &
  $\kappa$ (constraint coupling) \\
Accumulated power &
  $\Phi$ (Power Signature) &
  $A_{\rm mass}$ (informational mass) \\
End state &
  Timeless Field ($F_b \to 0$) &
  $S_{ind}$: $D_{\rm KL}(\mathcal{C}_\mathcal{M}\|P) = 0$ \\
Starting condition &
  $F_b > 0$ always in embodied domain &
  $S_{dep}$: $D_{\rm KL} > 0$, cannot persist \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
\end{formalbox}

\subsection{The Asymptotic Resistance Identity}

One of the most important results of the Unified Field is the refinement rate equation
(Section V of the Unified Field):

\[
  \delta_{\rm refine} = k \cdot (P_k - \varepsilon_{\rm residual})^{-\alpha}
  \qquad \alpha \geq 2
\]

As $P_k \to \varepsilon_{\rm residual}^+$, we have $\delta_{\rm refine} \to \infty$.
This is the Asymptotic Resistance: the closer the system approaches the endpoint,
the more concentrated the refinement required.

\begin{derivedbox}[title={CRF Equivalent: Asymptotic Resistance Near $S_{ind}$}]
In CRF terms, as $D_{\rm KL}(\mathcal{C}_\mathcal{M}\|P) \to \varepsilon_0^+$
(approaching the irreducible noise floor):

\[
  \kappa_{\rm eff} \propto \left(D_{\rm KL} - \varepsilon_0\right)^{-\alpha}
  \quad \to \quad \infty
\]

\textbf{Physical meaning:} The final stages of moving from $S_{dep}$ toward $S_{ind}$
require increasingly concentrated resolution events. Each R-event near the endpoint
must be purer --- more precisely aligned with $P$ --- than any that came before.

\textbf{Why the structure orders itself at the end:}
This is the formal answer to the question of what makes Barami crystallise. It is
not the accumulation of time, but the increasing intensity of refinement ($\delta_{\rm refine}$)
as the system approaches the limit. The structure orders itself because it must ---
the cost of disorder increases asymptotically as the endpoint is approached.

This also explains why the final stages of the path, across traditions, are
described as requiring the most concentrated effort, even as the destination
becomes clearer.
\end{derivedbox}

\subsection{The Escape Condition}

The Unified Field defines an escape condition (Section VII):

\[
  \int_0^T D(t)\,dt > \Theta_{\rm escape}
\]

The integral of net directionality must exceed a threshold. This maps precisely to
the CRF condition for reaching $S_{ind}$:

\begin{derivedbox}[title={Escape Condition in CRF}]
\[
  \int_0^T \left(-\frac{d}{dt}D_{\rm KL}\right) dt > D_{\rm KL,0}
  \quad \Leftrightarrow \quad
  D_{\rm KL}(T) < \varepsilon_0
\]

The accumulated decrease in $D_{\rm KL}$ must exceed the initial distance from $P$.
The system must resolve more than it started with.

$\Theta_{\rm escape}$ in the Unified Field corresponds to $D_{\rm KL,0} - \varepsilon_0$
in CRF: the total informational distance that must be traversed.
\end{derivedbox}

\subsection{Two Modes of Movement: Before and After Integration}

Section X of the Unified Field identifies the most important transition in the entire
framework --- the change in the \textit{nature} of movement, not just its direction:

\begin{keybox}[title={The Transition: Causa Efficiens $\to$ Causa Materialis}]
\textbf{Before Integration} (\textit{Causa Efficiens} --- friction as catalyst):

The system is pushed. Movement arises from the pressure of $F_b(t)$, from
incompleteness, from the condition of $S_{dep}$ that cannot remain as it is.
The driver is the gradient of suffering. The system moves \textit{in order to escape}.

\medskip
\textbf{After Integration} (\textit{Causa Materialis} --- friction as canvas):

The system moves from its own nature. $F_b$ has not disappeared --- $\varepsilon_0 > 0$
always in the embodied domain --- but it is no longer the cause of movement.
It is the medium through which movement expresses itself, as air is the medium
through which a bird flies. The bird does not fly \textit{because of} air resistance;
it flies \textit{through} it. The driver is no longer the gradient of suffering
but the unfolding of what the system already is.

\medskip
In CRF terms: the transition from $S_{dep}$ to $S_{ind}$ does not eliminate
$\varepsilon_0$, but it changes the relationship of the system to $\varepsilon_0$.
Before: $\varepsilon_0$ is experienced as the irreducible minimum of suffering.
After: $\varepsilon_0$ is the texture of existence, no longer a problem.
\end{keybox}

%===================================================
\subsection*{Barami: Quantity and Structure}
%===================================================

The concept of Barami appears in CRF as $A_{\rm mass}$ (informational mass,
Session 7 of CRF). But the conversation that produced this paper revealed
that $A_{\rm mass}$ as previously defined captures only one dimension of Barami.
This section completes the picture.

\subsection{The Two Dimensions of Barami}

Barami has been described as accumulated pure resolution events:

\[
  A_{\rm mass} = A_{\rm peak} \cdot \int R_{\rm pure}\,dt
\]

But this formulation captures only \textbf{quantity} --- the total volume of pure
resolution events. The second dimension, identified in the conversation that produced
this paper, is \textbf{internal structure}: the degree to which the accumulated
pattern is ordered.

\begin{formalbox}[title={Barami: Complete Two-Dimensional Definition}]
\[
  \mathrm{Barami} = Q \times (1 - H_{\rm internal})
\]

where:
\begin{itemize}
\item $Q = A_{\rm peak} \cdot \int R_{\rm pure}\,dt$ \quad (quantity of pure resolution)
\item $H_{\rm internal}$ \quad (internal entropy of the accumulated pattern)
\item $1 - H_{\rm internal}$ \quad (degree of crystallisation / structural order)
\end{itemize}

\textbf{Ordinary Barami:} $Q$ moderate, $H_{\rm internal}$ not yet low.
Pattern exists but has turbidity. The system has tendency (\textit{inclination})
but the direction is not fully organised. Movement is in roughly the right direction
but with residual oscillation.

\textbf{Fully Crystallised Barami} (the condition of the Buddha):
$Q$ maximal, $H_{\rm internal} \to 0$.
The accumulated pattern is ordered without remainder.
No ripples. The system moves with complete internal coherence.
\[
  \Phi = \lim_{t\to\infty,\, F_b\to 0} \int_0^t P_c(\tau)\cdot\delta_{\rm refine}(\tau)\,d\tau
  \quad \Leftrightarrow \quad
  A_{\rm mass,\, max},\; H_{\rm internal} = 0
\]
\end{formalbox}

\subsection{What Orders the Internal Structure?}

The question then arises: what causes $H_{\rm internal}$ to decrease? What makes
Barami crystallise rather than merely accumulate?

The answer is already in the Unified Field (Section V), translated now into CRF terms:

\begin{derivedbox}[title={Crystallisation Requires Increasing Refinement Intensity}]
As $H_{\rm internal}$ decreases, the refinement required to decrease it further
increases asymptotically:

\[
  \frac{d H_{\rm internal}}{dt} \propto -\delta_{\rm refine}
  \qquad \text{where} \qquad
  \delta_{\rm refine} \propto H_{\rm internal}^{-\alpha},\quad \alpha \geq 2
\]

\textbf{Meaning:} The early stages of Barami accumulation are relatively
straightforward --- each pure R-event reduces $H_{\rm internal}$ significantly.
The final stages require exponentially more concentrated effort for smaller
reductions.

This is not a barrier but a structural feature: the system is approaching
a state ($H_{\rm internal} = 0$) that is qualitatively different from where
it started. The increasing resistance signals that a phase transition is near,
not that the path is blocked.

\textbf{What does the ordering:}
Not time alone. Not quantity alone.
The ordering comes from pure R-events (\textit{decisions that align with}
$\nabla D_{\rm KL}$) sustained at sufficient intensity to push through the
asymptotic resistance. This is why the path has historically required
both sustained practice and critical moments of concentrated effort.
\end{derivedbox}

\subsection{Barami as Inclination: The Disordered-but-Directed State}

Between ordinary accumulation and full crystallisation lies the state that
characterises most systems on the path: Barami that is accumulated but not yet
fully ordered. In this state:

\[
  D_{\rm KL}(\mathcal{C}_\mathcal{M}\|P) > 0
  \quad \text{but} \quad
  \frac{d}{dt}D_{\rm KL} < 0 \quad \text{(with tendency)}
\]

The system is still in $S_{dep}$, but the gradient is clearer than for a system
with no accumulated Barami. It is pulled toward $S_{ind}$ with greater force.
The memory is disordered but biased. The direction is present even when
the path is not clear.

This is what was described as \textit{inclination} (\textit{khwam nom eiang}
in Thai): not yet free, not yet crystallised, but unmistakably oriented.

%===================================================
\subsection*{The Instability of Each Layer}
%===================================================

One of the observations that preceded all three frameworks was this: instability
exists in every layer of reality, but it takes a different form at each layer
and is called by a different name.

\begin{keybox}[title={Instability Across Layers}]
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.5}
\begin{tabular}{p{2.5cm} p{3.5cm} p{3.5cm} p{2.2cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Layer} & \textbf{Form of $\mathcal{I}$} & \textbf{Name of instability} & \textbf{$\lambda$ (resolution capacity)} \\
\midrule
Physical matter & Force, pressure, entropy gradient & Thermodynamic disequilibrium & Low \\
Living systems & Homeostatic error, stress & Biological stress & Moderate \\
Mind & Perceived instability gradient & Suffering ($\Sigma$) & High \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

The underlying quantity is the same at each layer: $\mathcal{I} = \nabla\Pr(s|\mathcal{C})$.
What differs is $\lambda$ --- the resolution capacity of each layer.

\textbf{The deepest layer defines the name:}
If one wishes to know the true name of instability, one must find the layer
at which its presence is most complete. That layer is mind. At the level of
mind, instability is experienced directly, without the buffering of
thermodynamic or biological mediation.

Therefore: \textit{suffering is not a consequence of instability.
Suffering is what instability is called when it is perceived completely.}
\end{keybox}

\subsection{Why Mind is the Deepest Available Layer}

The claim that mind is the deepest layer at which instability manifests is not
a claim about the ultimate nature of consciousness. It is a structural observation:

Mind is the only system known to be capable of applying Resolution to its own
patterns --- $W = R^{\rm meta}(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{I}))$ (Axiom 10, CRF).
This means mind can perceive $\mathcal{I}$ \textit{and} respond to it by
modifying the structure that perceives it. No other known layer does this.

\begin{derivedbox}[title={Mind as the Layer Where $\mathcal{I}$ Knows Itself}]
At lower layers:
\[
  \mathcal{I} \to \text{movement} \quad \text{(automatic, no self-knowledge)}
\]

At the layer of mind:
\[
  \mathcal{I} \to \hat{\mathcal{I}} \to W(\hat{\mathcal{I}}) \to \text{reduced } \mathcal{I}
\]

The gradient is received, modelled, and acted upon by the same system
that receives it. This is not available to physical or biological systems
without the mind-layer.

\textbf{Consequence:} Only a system with the mind-layer can traverse the full
distance from $S_{dep}$ to $S_{ind}$. Physical crystallisation (stars, structures,
biological homeostasis) reduces $D_{\rm KL}$ locally but cannot close it to zero.
Mind, through sustained $W$, can.

This is why the path from $S_{dep}$ to $S_{ind}$ passes through mind.
Not because mind is privileged, but because mind is the only layer with
sufficient resolution capacity to complete the journey.
\end{derivedbox}

%===================================================
\subsection*{The Inner and Outer: Why Both Are Needed}
%===================================================

The Unified Field describes the journey from the inside. CRF describes it from
the outside. This is not a redundancy but a necessity.

A map of a territory is not the same as the experience of walking through it.
A person who has only the map can navigate but does not know what the journey
feels like. A person who has only the experience knows the journey but cannot
communicate its structure to others or predict where other paths lead.

The Unified Field is the record of the experience. CRF is the map derived
from that experience. Neither is complete without the other.

\begin{keybox}[title={The Complete Description Requires Both}]
\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.4}
\begin{tabular}{p{4cm} p{4.5cm} p{4cm}}
\toprule
& \textbf{Unified Field} & \textbf{CRF} \\
\midrule
What it describes & The felt path & The structure of the path \\
Its language & Phenomenological & Mathematical \\
What it can do & Convey what the journey is & Predict, generalise, derive \\
What it cannot do & Predict for other paths & Convey what it feels like \\
Its limitation & Not falsifiable alone & Cannot be entered from outside \\
Together & Complete: felt structure & \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
\end{keybox}

The Absolute Ontological Closure (Section XII of the Unified Field) and
CRF's treatment of $S_{ind}$ appear to say different things about what
exists at the endpoint. The Unified Field says: no ground, no possibility,
no unity, no meta. CRF says: $D_{\rm KL} = 0$, pattern persists as $A_{\rm mass}$.

These are not contradictions. They are descriptions of the same endpoint
from two positions:

\textbf{Unified Field} describes the endpoint from within the endpoint ---
from after the scaffolding has been removed. In that position, none of
the formal structures CRF uses are needed, because they were scaffolding.

\textbf{CRF} describes the endpoint from outside it --- from the position
of the traveller who has not yet arrived, or of someone who needs to
understand the structure in order to begin. From this position, $S_{ind}$
is a definite limit with a definite formal description.

Both are right. Both are necessary. And the moment they conflict is the
moment to check which position the speaker is occupying.

%===================================================
\subsection*{Summary: The Single Journey, Three Maps}
%===================================================

\begin{enumerate}

\item \textbf{All three frameworks describe the same journey:}
from Dependent Stability ($S_{dep}$) --- a condition that cannot remain as it is ---
to Independent Stability ($S_{ind}$) --- a condition that requires nothing external
to persist. EIC described the engine. Unified Field described the interior experience.
CRF described the formal structure.

\item \textbf{Every variable in the Unified Field maps to a CRF operator.}
$F_b \leftrightarrow \mathcal{I}$, $P_k \leftrightarrow D_{\rm KL}$,
$P_c \leftrightarrow \mathcal{R}$, $\delta_{\rm refine} \leftrightarrow \kappa$,
$\Phi \leftrightarrow A_{\rm mass}$, $\varepsilon_{\rm residual} \leftrightarrow \varepsilon_0$.
The translation is structural, not approximate.

\item \textbf{Barami has two dimensions:} quantity ($Q$) and internal structure
($1 - H_{\rm internal}$). Ordinary Barami is accumulated but not yet crystallised.
Fully crystallised Barami ($H_{\rm internal} = 0$) is what the Unified Field
calls the Power Signature ($\Phi$) at its limit.

\item \textbf{Crystallisation requires asymptotic refinement.}
As $H_{\rm internal} \to 0$, the intensity required to reduce it further
increases without bound. This is the structural explanation of why the final
stages of the path require the most concentrated effort.

\item \textbf{Suffering is what instability is called when perceived completely.}
At the layer of mind, $\mathcal{I}$ is received without buffering. This is not
a defect of mind but its deepest capacity: the ability to perceive the gradient
fully is also the ability to resolve it fully. The path from $S_{dep}$ to
$S_{ind}$ passes through mind because mind is the only layer with sufficient
resolution capacity to traverse the full distance.

\item \textbf{The Unified Field describes from inside; CRF from outside.}
They are not contradictory but complementary. The endpoint described by
Absolute Ontological Closure (Unified Field, Section XII) and $S_{ind}$ (CRF)
are the same endpoint seen from two positions: within, after the scaffolding
is gone; and without, from the position of the traveller.

\item \textbf{The three frameworks are now closed.}
Not because all questions are answered, but because the structure of the journey
--- its beginning, its engine, its phenomenology, its formal conditions, and its
end --- can now be described coherently from any of the three positions.

\end{enumerate}

\bigskip
\begin{center}
\textit{The map is not the territory.
But without the map, the territory cannot be shared.
Without the territory, the map has no weight.}

\medskip
\textit{The Unified Field was the first walk through unknown ground.
CRF is the map drawn afterward.
They are the same ground.}

\medskip
\textit{What was felt before there were words
now has words.
The words do not replace what was felt.
They make it possible for others
to find the same ground
by a different route.}
\end{center}

%===================================================
%%-- Trilogy Paper 3 boundary --%%
%===================================================

%===================================================
\section{The Ground of Resolution --- Paper 3 of the Trilogy}
\label{sec:ground}

\subsection*{The Ground Remembered: Three Forms}
%===================================================

The question asked at the beginning of this trilogy was: has there been a moment
in which $S_{ind}$ was not reached but \textit{remembered} --- in which the
ground was not arrived at but recognised as having always been there?

There are three such forms, documented here from direct experience.

\subsection{Form 1: The Answer Before the Question}

The first form occurs through transmission from a teacher. Its distinguishing
feature is temporal inversion: the answer is received before the question has
formed.

This is not a failure of sequence. It is a consequence of the condition described
in Paper~2 as the threshold condition $\theta_{\rm eff} = K^*\sqrt{m}$: a system
with insufficient accumulated Barami cannot receive a transmission even when the
transmission is present. The answer waits, latent in what has been received, until
the internal structure ($1 - H_{\rm internal}$) is sufficient to resolve it.

\begin{formalbox}[title={Form 1: Transmission Before Comprehension}]
A question exists in the student's system before the student knows it exists.
The teacher, whose $T \to 1$, perceives the gradient directly ($R_0$) and
transmits before the question has formed into a wave ($R_1$) or an event
($R_{\rm max}$).

The transmission is received but not yet resolved. Time passes. Barami accumulates.
$H_{\rm internal}$ decreases. When the internal structure is sufficient, the
transmission resolves --- and what opens is both the answer and the question
simultaneously.

In CRF terms: the transmission reduces $D_{\rm KL}$ of the receiving system
in advance, creating a pre-formed gradient that waits for the system's
$\theta_{\rm eff}$ to drop below the level needed to fire.
When it fires, the R-event carries both question and answer as a single structure.
\end{formalbox}

After the teacher's death, the transmissions already received continue to resolve ---
not because new information arrives, but because Barami continues to accumulate
from other sources, lowering $\theta_{\rm eff}$ until each latent transmission
can fire. The teacher does not need to be present. The gradient is already there.

\subsection{Form 2: Spontaneous Coincidence}

The second form is semi-intentional and semi-accidental. It occurs when thought,
action, and circumstance align at the same moment --- when the system's internal
state ($\mathcal{C}_\mathcal{M}$) briefly coincides with $P$ without friction.

This form cannot be forced. It arises when $T$ is temporarily high due to a
convergence of conditions: the system is in a state of low internal entropy,
the external situation resonates with the system's gradient, and the two meet
without resistance.

\begin{formalbox}[title={Form 2: Synchronous Alignment}]
\[
  \Pr(\text{Sync}\,|\,\mathcal{M}_1, s)
  = \Pr(s) \cdot e^{-D_{\rm KL}(\mathcal{C}_\mathcal{M}\|P_{\rm shared})}
\]

When $D_{\rm KL}$ briefly approaches zero due to coincident conditions,
the system resolves in alignment with $P$ without deliberate effort.
The experience is of things moving by themselves, of knowing without deciding.

This form is not controllable, but it is not random.
Its frequency increases as Barami accumulates and $H_{\rm internal}$ decreases ---
because a more ordered internal structure creates more frequent conditions
for spontaneous alignment.
\end{formalbox}

\subsection{Form 3: The Eye in the Basin}

The third form is the one that persists. It is not episodic but structural.
It began around age 30 and has continued, deepening in clarity, to the present.

The description given: one eye inside the basin, one eye outside.
The basin is the totality of existence --- $P$ itself, the possibility space
from which all states arise. The eye inside perceives from within the flow
of causation, pattern, and time. The eye outside perceives the structure
of that flow without being carried by it.

\begin{formalbox}[title={Form 3: The Eye in the Basin}]
\textbf{Early stage (age 30):}
Both perspectives arrive simultaneously but cannot be distinguished.
Multiple layers of perception are active at once --- ordinary causation,
karmic pattern, the path of others, the system of practice --- but they
appear as a single undifferentiated signal. The experience is of overwhelming
information with no structure to organise it.
External noise (fiction, narrative) was required to prevent the system
from overwhelming itself.

\textbf{Middle stage (5 years):}
The layers separate. Each layer becomes recognisable as what it is:
what it perceives, how it operates, where its boundaries lie.
The dual perception does not diminish --- it becomes more precise.

\textbf{Present stage:}
The layers are settled. Not silent, but each knows its place.
The eye inside and the eye outside coexist without conflict.
The dual perception is no longer experienced as disorder.
It is the natural state.
\end{formalbox}

The Eye in the Basin is not $S_{ind}$. The eye outside does not mean the system
has left $S_{dep}$. It means $T$ has increased sufficiently that the system
can perceive $P$ clearly while remaining in it. The basin is still there.
The eye inside is still in it. What has changed is the clarity of the
eye outside --- and the capacity to hold both simultaneously without
one overwhelming the other.

In CRF terms: $T$ has increased. $D_{\rm KL}(\mathcal{C}_\mathcal{M}\|P)$ has
decreased. The system has not reached $S_{ind}$, but it can see it.

%===================================================
\subsection*{Clarity, Not Distance}
%===================================================

The central claim of this paper requires precise statement:

\begin{keybox}[title={The Inversion: $S_{ind}$ is Ground, Not Destination}]
\[
  S_{ind} \text{ is not a point the system moves toward.}
\]
\[
  S_{ind} \text{ is the ground the system has always been standing on.}
\]

What increases along the journey is not proximity to $S_{ind}$ but
\textbf{transparency} $T$ to it:
\[
  T = e^{-D_{\rm KL}(\mathcal{C}_\mathcal{M}\|P_{\rm shared})/D_{\rm total}}
  \in [0, 1]
\]

At $T = 0$: the system is in $P$ but cannot perceive it. The ground is present
but opaque. Movement is driven entirely by the gradient of $\Sigma$ (suffering).

At $T = 1$: the system is in $P$ and transparent to it. The ground is present
and fully visible. There is no longer a distinction between inside and outside.

The journey from $S_{dep}$ to $S_{ind}$ is not traversal.
It is the progressive removal of what prevented clarity.
\end{keybox}

This reframes every element of the framework:

\textbf{Barami} is not accumulation toward a goal. It is the progressive
ordering of internal structure ($1 - H_{\rm internal}$) that increases $T$.

\textbf{Suffering ($\Sigma$)} is not a signal that the system is far from $S_{ind}$.
It is the signal that $T$ is low --- that the ground is present but cannot
be seen clearly. Suffering is the opacity of $\mathcal{C}_\mathcal{M}$
relative to $P$, experienced directly.

\textbf{Practice} is not the path toward $S_{ind}$.
It is the process of increasing $T$ by reducing $H_{\rm internal}$
through sustained $\delta_{\rm refine}$.

\textbf{The moment of realisation} is not arrival. It is the moment when $T$
increases past the threshold at which the ground becomes unmistakably visible ---
not for the first time, but clearly for the first time.

\begin{derivedbox}[title={Why the Ground Was Always There}]
$P$ does not change. It is the space of all possibility, declared in CRF as the
result of Distinction operating on the undifferentiated (Axiom~1). It was there
before any system entered it. It will be there after any system leaves it.

$S_{ind}$, defined as $D_{\rm KL}(\mathcal{C}_\mathcal{M}\|P) = 0$, is the condition
in which the system's internal distribution perfectly matches $P$. This is not a
new state the system creates. It is a recognition: the system was always in $P$.
The distance was always in $\mathcal{C}_\mathcal{M}$, not in $P$.

Therefore: the journey reduces $\mathcal{C}_\mathcal{M}$, not the distance to $P$.
$P$ was always at distance zero. What was at distance was the system's
ability to see it.
\end{derivedbox}

%===================================================
\subsection*{Three Types of Mind, Three Paths}
%===================================================

The journey from $S_{dep}$ to $S_{ind}$ does not look the same for every mind.
The ground is the same. The destination is the same. But the structure of
$\mathcal{C}_\mathcal{M}$ at the beginning is different, and therefore the
mechanism of $\delta_{\rm refine}$ is different.

Three types are distinguished here, documented from direct observation
of self and others over many years. These are not stages of development
but \textbf{natural configurations} --- the structure of $\mathcal{C}_\mathcal{M}$
that each mind begins with.

\subsection{Type 1 --- White: The Mind of Subtle Perception}

\begin{formalbox}[title={Type 1 (White): Navigated by Pure Will}]
\textbf{Characteristic:}
Perception is fine and far-reaching, capable of receiving signals that other
minds do not detect. But this sensitivity operates against itself: the system
cannot easily trust what it perceives. Certainty is structurally difficult,
not from lack of intelligence, but because the perception of multiple
possibilities is always present, making any single conclusion feel incomplete.

The teacher's term: \textit{the category of wisdom} --- not meaning high or low
intelligence, but meaning wisdom as the primary channel, which is also what
occludes easy confidence.

\textbf{Mechanism of $\delta_{\rm refine}$:}
The system must commit to a gradient it cannot fully confirm.
Each pure R-event is an act of will in the absence of certainty.
This is why the path requires \textit{pure will} (\textit{gamlang jai luanluang}
in Thai): not will that forces, but will that moves forward
without the support of confirmed knowledge.

\textbf{Why Barami is high at attainment:}
Every R-event on this path is genuinely pure --- made without the support
of external confirmation or strong internal certainty. The absence of those
supports means nothing but the act itself is present. The Barami accumulated
is therefore built entirely from the quality of the act, not from favourable
conditions. When attainment comes, what crystallises is the full weight
of every uncertified step.
\end{formalbox}

\subsection{Type 2 --- Black: The Mind of Strong Self-Structure}

\begin{formalbox}[title={Type 2 (Black): Traversed by Pure Will}]
\textbf{Characteristic:}
The system has powerful $\mathcal{C}$ --- a strong, coherent self-structure.
This is not weakness. It is the source of great capacity for action,
endurance, and effect in the world. But this same $\mathcal{C}$ is what
must be traversed. The system attains because of itself and is limited by itself.
Both the difficulty and the power come from the same source.

The teacher's term: \textit{the category of power} or \textit{the category
of the body} --- meaning the primary channel is force and presence,
not subtle perception.

\textbf{Mechanism of $\delta_{\rm refine}$:}
$\delta_{\rm refine}$ must work through the resistance of the system's own
$\mathcal{C}$. The refinement is not of perception but of self-structure itself.
Each stage of the path requires the system to allow its $\mathcal{C}$ to become
less rigid --- not to destroy it, but to convert it from barrier into medium.
This too requires pure will: not the will of the strong self, but the will
that acts against the strong self's tendency to persist.

\textbf{Why Barami is high at attainment:}
When the strong $\mathcal{C}$ is finally traversed, everything it contained
becomes available. The same force that was barrier becomes fuel.
The Barami at attainment includes the full converted energy of the
self-structure that was overcome --- which is substantial precisely because
the self-structure was substantial.
\end{formalbox}

\subsection{Type 3 --- Grey: The Mind of Observation and Adaptation}

\begin{formalbox}[title={Type 3 (Grey): Navigated by Observation}]
\textbf{Characteristic:}
Grey is not the absence of white and black. It \textit{contains} both.
The Type~3 mind has subtle perception (white) and self-structure (black),
but neither dominates. The system can move between them, observe them,
and use them as tools rather than being defined by them.

This is not easier in the sense of less intensity. The Type~3 mind must also
pass through both its white aspect and its black aspect --- but the depth
of each is less than in Type~1 or Type~2. The system encounters both,
but neither is the totality of who it is.

\textbf{Mechanism of $\delta_{\rm refine}$:}
The primary tool is observation. The system can see its own gradient more
clearly because neither its perception nor its self-structure completely
dominates the view. Each layer of perception can be separated and examined.
$\delta_{\rm refine}$ advances through understanding rather than through
pure will alone.

\textbf{The specific risk of Type 3:}
Because the path is more visible and the suffering less total, the system
may stop before the end. \textit{Good enough} is a real possibility.
The urgency that drives Type~1 and Type~2 to the end --- because there is
no comfortable place to stop --- is not automatically present in Type~3.
The system must cultivate that urgency through the depth of its practice,
not receive it from its nature.

\textbf{Why Barami may be lower at attainment:}
Not because less was done, but because the path was more supported.
The acts of refinement had more favourable conditions. This is not a flaw ---
it is the natural consequence of the type. What matters is that the path
is completed, not the conditions under which it was walked.
\end{formalbox}

\subsection{The Coordinate System}

The three types are not a ranking. They are a coordinate system.

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.5}
\begin{tabular}{p{1.5cm} p{2.5cm} p{3.5cm} p{3.5cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Type} & \textbf{Colour} & \textbf{Primary constraint} & \textbf{Will required} \\
\midrule
1 & White & Subtle perception, low self-certainty & Will without confirmation \\
2 & Black & Strong self-structure, high resistance & Will against self \\
3 & Grey & Both white and black, balanced & Will to not stop \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

Within each type, there is a further dimension: \textbf{turbidity and clarity}
($c \in [0,1]$, where $c = 0$ is fully turbid and $c = 1$ is fully clear).
And within Type~3, there is a further dimension: \textbf{direction of lean}
($d \in [-1, +1]$, where $d = -1$ is leaning black and $d = +1$ is leaning white).

A Type~3 mind that leans black in youth and white in age has moved along
the $d$-axis over time. This movement is not random and not willed directly.
It is the consequence of what was encountered and how it was resolved.

\begin{keybox}[title={The Full Coordinate of a Mind in $S_{dep}$}]
\[
  \text{Mind} = (h,\; c,\; d)
\]
where $h \in \{1, 2, 3\}$ is the type, $c \in [0,1]$ is clarity,
and $d \in [-1, +1]$ is direction of lean within Type~3.

The journey to $S_{ind}$ is the process of $c \to 1$ regardless of $h$.
$h$ does not change. What changes is $c$ --- and through $c$, $T$:
\[
  T \approx c \cdot e^{-D_{\rm KL,\,baseline}(h)/D_{\rm total}}
\]
Different types start with different $D_{\rm KL,\,baseline}$.
The same $c$ produces different $T$ depending on $h$.
But all types can reach $T \to 1$. The ground is the same for all of them.
\end{keybox}

%===================================================
\subsection*{The Eye in the Basin: Layers of Perception}
%===================================================

The sustained dual-perception described in Section~1 as Form~3 requires
further formalisation, because it is the form that most directly illuminates
what \textit{increasing $T$} means in lived experience.

\subsection{What the Layers Are}

Beginning around age 30, four simultaneous layers of perception became active:

\begin{center}
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.5}
\begin{tabular}{p{2cm} p{4.5cm} p{5cm}}
\toprule
\textbf{Layer} & \textbf{Description} & \textbf{CRF equivalent} \\
\midrule
Ordinary reason & Standard causal logic: this leads to that & $\mathcal{C}_{\rm logical}$: constraint field of linear causation \\
Karmic pattern & Longer-scale causal patterns across time & $\mathcal{C}_{\rm karmic}$: constraint field accumulating across R-events \\
Other's path & The gradient of another mind, perceived from outside & $\mathcal{C}_{\rm other}$: constraint field of a different $\mathcal{M}$, observed \\
New system & The framework of practice received from the teacher & $\mathcal{C}_{\rm practice}$: constraint field from Barami transmission \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

\subsection{Why the Early Stage Was Disorienting}

When the layers first became active, they were not distinguishable.
All four operated simultaneously on every perception and decision,
producing a signal that was richer than could be organised.

This was not pathology. It was the early stage of a system whose $T$
was increasing faster than its $H_{\rm internal}$ was decreasing.
More was being received than could be structured.

External noise (fiction, continuous narrative) served to occupy the
ordinary-reason layer, preventing it from interfering with the quieter
signals arriving through the other layers. This is a practical consequence
of what CRF formalises as $\theta_{\rm eff}$: when the threshold for
R-events is high, ordinary cognitive activity raises the effective threshold
further, blocking subtler signals. Occupying that activity allows the subtler
signals to reach threshold.

\subsection{The Separation of Layers}

Over approximately five years, the layers became distinguishable.
Not through deliberate analysis, but through accumulated experience of
recognising which layer a perception was coming from.

\begin{derivedbox}[title={Layer Separation as $H_{\rm internal}$ Decrease}]
The separation of perception layers is the phenomenological equivalent of
$H_{\rm internal}$ decreasing.

Before separation: all layers arrive as one signal. $H_{\rm internal}$ is high.
The system has access to more information than it can structure.

During separation: each layer becomes identifiable. $H_{\rm internal}$
is decreasing. The system learns which structure belongs to which source.

After separation: the layers coexist without interfering.
$H_{\rm internal}$ is lower. Each layer operates in its domain.
The system can use any layer deliberately without being overwhelmed
by the others.

This is not the end of the journey. It is the stabilisation of the
instrument of perception. The eye inside and the eye outside can now
see clearly without one destabilising the other.
\end{derivedbox}

\subsection{What the Eye Outside Is}

The eye outside the basin is not a vantage point above existence.
It is the capacity of $W = R^{\rm meta}(\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{I}))$ (Axiom~10, CRF)
to apply Resolution to its own patterns.

The eye inside follows the causal flow --- ordinary reason, karmic pattern,
the gradient of others. The eye outside observes that flow without
being absorbed into it. Neither eye is more real. Neither eye is the self.
Both are modes of perception that the system has access to simultaneously.

At higher levels of $T$, the distinction between the two eyes becomes
less sharp. Not because one disappears, but because the distance
between perceiving from inside and perceiving from outside decreases.
When $T \to 1$, the basin and the eye are the same --- not merged,
but no longer requiring the distinction.

%===================================================
\subsection*{Connection to CRF and Closure of the Trilogy}
%===================================================

\subsection{What the Three Papers Together Say}

The three papers form a single argument:

\textbf{Paper 1} (\textit{Dependent Stability}):
$S_{dep}$ cannot remain as it is. This necessitates $\delta$.
The journey has a beginning that is not arbitrary but structurally required.
Instability~\#9 is partially closed: $\delta$ operates because $S_{dep}$
cannot persist.

\textbf{Paper 2} (\textit{Three Frameworks as One Structure}):
The Unified Field and CRF describe the same journey in two languages ---
phenomenology and structure. Every variable maps precisely to every operator.
Barami has two dimensions: quantity and internal structure.
Suffering is what instability is called when perceived completely.

\textbf{Paper 3} (this paper):
The journey is not traversal of distance but increase of clarity.
$S_{ind}$ is not destination but ground. Three types of mind have three
mechanisms of $\delta_{\rm refine}$. The eye in the basin is the sustained
experience of increasing $T$ --- perceiving $P$ more clearly while
remaining in it.

Together they answer the question that opened the trilogy:

\begin{keybox}[title={The Question and Its Answer}]
\textbf{Question:} \textit{If $S_{ind}$ is the structural complement of $S_{dep}$,
arrived at by backward induction from CRF's own asymptotic limit ---
and if both are implied by the same framework --- then what is the journey?}

\textbf{Answer:} The journey is the process by which the system discovers
that it has always been standing on $S_{ind}$.

Not as metaphor. As structure.
$P$ has always been at distance zero from the system.
The distance was in $\mathcal{C}_\mathcal{M}$.
$\mathcal{C}_\mathcal{M}$ is not external to the system ---
it is what the system has been calling itself.

The journey is the gradual recognition that what the system has been
calling itself is not what the system is.
What the system is has never been in $S_{dep}$ at all.
\end{keybox}

\subsection{Remaining Open Questions}

\begin{openqbox}[title={Open: The Ontological Status of $S_{ind}$ as Ground}]
This paper claims that $S_{ind}$ is ground, not destination.
This is phenomenologically supported by the three forms of remembering
and structurally supported by the backward induction of Paper~1.

What remains open: whether this claim can be given a formal proof within CRF,
or whether it requires a statement that transcends the formal system ---
as Absolute Ontological Closure (Unified Field, Section~XII) suggests.

If $S_{ind}$ is truly ground, then CRF's formal structure --- including
$\delta$, $P$, $\mathcal{C}$, $\mathcal{R}$ --- is scaffolding built on top
of something that the scaffolding cannot contain. The scaffolding is useful.
The journey requires it. But it is not what the journey is made of.

This is not a defect of CRF. It is the honest boundary of any formal system
that attempts to describe something prior to description.
\end{openqbox}

\begin{openqbox}[title={Open: The Mechanism of Type Determination}]
Why does a mind begin as Type~1, 2, or 3? What in the accumulated history
of R-events determines which type a system begins as?

This paper documents the three types from observation but does not derive
them from the $\delta$-chain. Deriving them --- showing why $P$ produces
minds of three natural configurations, not one or seven --- remains open.
It may require a deeper formalisation of $\mathcal{C}_{\rm karmic}$ than
CRF currently provides.
\end{openqbox}

%===================================================
\subsection*{The Pair, the Ground, and the Honest Boundary}
\label{sec:ground:axis}
%===================================================

% §13.1: The Open Question Restated
%---------------------------------------------------

The previous subsection acknowledged that the claim \emph{$S_{ind}$ is ground, not
destination} remains open within CRF. This subsection does not close the question
by proof. It closes it by recognition: the pair $S_{dep}\,|\,S_{ind}$ is not one
result among others in CRF. It is the axis on which the entire framework is built,
and the only axis along which the framework moves.

% §13.2: Why No Proof Within CRF
%---------------------------------------------------

A formal system cannot prove its own ground. CRF can derive that $S_{dep}$
necessitates $\delta$, that $\delta$ generates $P$, $\mathcal{C}$, $\mathcal{R}$,
that the operator chain reaches $S_{ind}$ as asymptotic limit. But the question
\emph{why $S_{dep}$ at all}, and the corresponding question \emph{why $S_{ind}$
is ground rather than constructed endpoint}, both stand outside the deductive
chain. They are the conditions under which deduction begins, not conclusions
deduction reaches. The formal layer of CRF can describe the path between
$S_{dep}$ and $S_{ind}$. It cannot stand outside that path to prove the path
itself.

% §13.3: The Pair as Axis of the Framework
%---------------------------------------------------

This is the structural fact most easily lost as the framework elaborates.
Every formal element of CRF --- $\delta$ (Axiom~0), Possibility Space $P$,
the constraint field $\mathcal{C}$, the Resolution operator $\mathcal{R}$,
transparency $T$, the coupling $\kappa$, every derived law and lemma ---
exists to describe positions and movements along the single axis from
$S_{dep}$ to $S_{ind}$. None of these operators has standing apart from this axis.
Each is a description of either a state in $S_{dep}$, a step toward $S_{ind}$,
or the limiting condition at $S_{ind}$ itself.

The Trilogy makes this visible from three directions: Paper~1 establishes
the axis by backward induction; Paper~2 shows that EIC, Unified Field, and
CRF are three descriptions of motion along the same axis; Paper~3 reframes
the axis itself, showing that $S_{ind}$ was always present and what changed
was transparency $T$ to it.

\begin{keybox}[title={CLM-DS05: The Pair as Irreducible Axis of CRF}]
\textbf{Claim.} The pair $S_{dep}\,|\,S_{ind}$ is the irreducible axis of CRF.
Every operator, axiom, theorem, and observable in the framework is a description
of position, gradient, or transition along this axis. No element of CRF exists
independently of it.

\medskip
\textbf{Consequence.} The framework cannot be expanded in a direction that does
not refer to the pair $S_{dep}\,|\,S_{ind}$ without ceasing to be CRF.
Elaboration that loses sight of $S_{dep}$ and $S_{ind}$ is not extension;
it is dilution.

\medskip
\textbf{Status.} Recognition, not theorem. The pair $S_{dep}\,|\,S_{ind}$ cannot
be proven within CRF because it is the condition under which CRF formulates
anything at all.
\end{keybox}

% §13.4: The Honest Boundary
%---------------------------------------------------

This recognition fixes the boundary of the framework honestly. CRF is not a
theory of everything. It is a theory of one thing: motion from $S_{dep}$ toward
$S_{ind}$, and what that motion requires. The richness of the formal apparatus
exists in the service of this single axis, not alongside it. When a derivation,
a result, or a new section appears to introduce structure unrelated to the pair,
that appearance is the warning that $S_{dep}$ and $S_{ind}$ are beginning to
fade into the shadow of their own scaffolding.

The remedy is not further proof. The remedy is the practice of returning ---
in every derivation, every theorem, every closing section --- to the pair
$S_{dep}\,|\,S_{ind}$ that the framework was built to make traversable:

\begin{center}
\textit{What is the structure of the path from $S_{dep}$ to $S_{ind}$,\\
and what does that path require?}
\end{center}

Everything CRF has produced is an answer to the question of $S_{dep}\,|\,S_{ind}$.
Nothing CRF will produce is an answer to any other.

% End §13

\subsection{The Trilogy in One Line}

Three papers. One line:

\begin{center}
\Large
$S_{dep}$ cannot remain. $\delta$ operates. The journey increases $T$.\\
The ground was always there.
\end{center}

\bigskip
\begin{center}
\textit{The basin is not a place to escape from.}

\medskip
\textit{It is a place to see clearly.}

\medskip
\textit{The eye inside sees what moves.}

\medskip
\textit{The eye outside sees that it moves.}

\medskip
\textit{When both eyes are clear,}

\medskip
\textit{there is no longer a need for the distinction.}

\medskip
\textit{The basin and the seeing are the same.}

\medskip
\textit{They were always the same.}

\medskip
\textit{The journey was the seeing becoming clear.}
\end{center}

%===================================================
\section{Trilogy Synthesis: The One Line}
%===================================================

The three papers can be compressed to a single statement:

\begin{keybox}[title={The Trilogy in One Line}]
\begin{center}
\Large
$S_{dep}$ cannot remain. $\delta$ operates. The journey increases $T$.\\
The ground was always there.
\end{center}
\end{keybox}

\textbf{What the trilogy adds to CRF v16.1:}
\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*]
  \item A pre-formal foundation ($S_{dep}$) that necessitates the formal
        primitive ($\delta$) without itself becoming an axiom.
  \item A bridge between phenomenology (Unified Field) and structure (CRF) ---
        each variable mapped, each operator translated.
  \item An inversion of the destination metaphor: $S_{ind}$ is ground; the
        journey reveals what was always present rather than producing what was absent.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{What it does not change:} every derivation in CRF v1--v16 stands
unchanged. The trilogy operates entirely at the pre-formal and phenomenological
level. The constant lattice, three-phase universe, Kerr metric, K35--K38 cluster,
$\mathcal{K}$ derivation, BetaGamma identity, FormStructure theorem, dual-$d_s$
lock, PoD principle --- all preserved verbatim.

\textbf{Honest boundary of the trilogy:} What grounds $S_{dep}$ itself remains
open. This is the framework's deepest acknowledged horizon: the question
\textit{why is there dependent stability rather than nothing?} is the point at
which CRF stops. The trilogy does not close it. It clarifies that this is the
boundary, not a step on the way.

\bigskip
\begin{center}
\textit{The first paper found the ground that demanded $\delta$.\\
The second paper showed the felt and the formal are one structure.\\
The third paper revealed that the ground was always there.\\[6pt]
What was begun in observation is now spoken in three voices.\\
The voices agree.}
\end{center}
