\section{Noether $\mathbb{Z}_{15}$ --- Paper 4 of the Extended Trilogy (v17.1)}
%===================================================
% Gauge: T-canonical (d_s = 3 exact). Finite-N corrections tagged [E-gauge].
% This section condenses CRF_Noether_Z15_StepD_revised.tex (1116 lines, 16-page PDF)
% into the v17.1 master while preserving all atomic units and the standalone-paper-first
% protocol (CLM-AM04). The standalone PDF remains the canonical full source;
% this section provides the integrated narrative with full atomic registry.

The fourth paper of the Extended Trilogy resumes the symmetry programme.
Where Papers 1--3 grounded CRF in pre-formal observation, framework
unification, and the inversion of the destination metaphor, Paper~4
returns to the algebraic question: \emph{what symmetry conserves
$\beta$?} The draft Noether Z15 paper proposed three layers of
argument, each with one open gap. Step D of the v17 Methodology
Programme re-derived the Noether structure under T-canonical and
found that the third gap (a $0.5\%$ residual misattributed to a
Phase~II $\theta_{\rm eff}(m)$ correction) was a Silent Gauge Drift
artefact. The anchor switch to the BetaGamma identity tightens the
Layer~2 prediction by approximately three orders of magnitude.

\subsection{The Anchor Switch (Layer~2)}

\begin{formalbox}[title={DEF-NZ01: T-Canonical Noether Anchor for $\beta$}]
The conserved $\Psi$-translation charge in the Noether programme is
identified with the BetaGamma combination:
\[
  \boxed{\beta \;=\; d_s \cdot \mathcal{G} \;=\; d_s \cdot \frac{\alpha}{D_0}}
\]
where $d_s = 3$ (T-canonical, integer count of spatial symmetric directions
in $\mathrm{Cl}(3,0)$), $\alpha = \ln 2$, and $D_0 = n(1-e^{-1/n})$.
Numerically: $\beta_T = 2.2121135$ at full precision.

\textbf{Interpretation.} $\mathcal{G} = \alpha/D_0$ is the universal
per-direction coupling (gauge-invariant; does not contain $d_s$);
$d_s = 3$ counts the integer-many T-home symmetric directions; their
product is the $\Psi$-charge each $\mathcal{R}$-event resolves.
\end{formalbox}

\begin{derivedbox}[title={EQ-NZ01--05: Layer~2 Numerical Lattice}]
Under T-canonical, the Layer~2 anchor lattice reads:
\begin{align}
\beta_{\rm sim}\;(\text{V20.3, }N=500\text{, 4-run, 5-digit}) &= 2.21210
   \tag{EQ-NZ01}\\
\beta_T = d_s\mathcal{G}\;(\text{T-canonical}) &= 2.2121135
   \tag{EQ-NZ02}\\
J_\beta^{(\rm A)} = F/\alpha\;(\text{Noether v1, E-gauge implicit}) &= 2.2015526
   \tag{EQ-NZ03}\\
\text{gap}(\beta_T,\,\beta_{\rm sim}) &= 0.0006\%
   \tag{EQ-NZ04}\\
\text{tightening over }J_\beta^{(\rm A)} &\approx 779\times \;(\sim 800\times)
   \tag{EQ-NZ05}
\end{align}
The previously published gap of $0.048\%$ is the same algebraic object
read against $4$-digit $\beta_{\rm sim}$ with chained roundings;
$0.0006\%$ is the same gap read at $5$-digit precision (FIND-AM01).
Both readings are valid; the precision of the comparison baseline
differs.
\end{derivedbox}

\begin{claim}[CLM-NZ01: BetaGamma is the Symmetry-Respecting Anchor]
The $\Psi$-translation Noether current, when re-anchored on
$\beta = d_s\mathcal{G}$ rather than on $J_\beta = F/\alpha$, agrees
with the V20.3 simulation to $0.0006\%$ at $5$-digit precision
($\sim 800\times$ tightening over the original anchor). The original
anchor's $0.49\%$ residual was a Silent Gauge Drift artefact (CUIP
Failure Mode 7), not a Phase~II $\theta_{\rm eff}(m)$ correction.
\end{claim}

\subsection{Layer~1 (Equipartition) --- Clarified, Not Replaced}

The Layer~1 equipartition argument is preserved with explicit gauge
tagging:

\begin{formalbox}[title={DEF-NZ02: Equipartition under Dual Gauge}]
The phase-variance equipartition statement reads:
\begin{description}[leftmargin=1.2cm,style=nextline]
  \item[T-gauge (thermo-limit, exact)]
        $\varphi_{\rm var}^{(T)} = 1/2$ as $N\to\infty$.
  \item[E-gauge (finite-$N$ via K7$'$)]
        $\varphi_{\rm var}^{(E)} = 1/2 - \sqrt{d_s}/n^2 = 0.47280$
        at $d_s = 3.031$, $n = 8$, $N = 500$.
\end{description}
The K7$'$ correction encodes the leading finite-$N$ residual; the
T-gauge statement is its $N\to\infty$ limit. Both refer to the same
underlying observable, distinguished by the precision regime.
\end{formalbox}

\begin{claim}[CLM-NZ02: Layer~1 Gauge Tagging Resolves Verification Drift]
The K7$'$ identity ($\varphi_{\rm var} = 1/2 - \sqrt{d_s}/n^2$) is
E-home (finite-$N$ matched), with E-gauge gap $0.0206\%$ at $d_s=3.031$
and T-gauge gap $0.0501\%$ at $d_s=3$. Identity~\#4 of the v17
Reclassification Table classifies this as E-home; the v17 verification
suite (Check~9, gap $0.0217\%$) confirms.
\end{claim}

\subsection{Layer~3 ($\mathbb{Z}_{15}$ Conjecture) --- \textbf{Closed in v17.3 as THM-Z101}}

\begin{formalbox}[title={CONJ-NZ01 $\to$ THM-Z101: Integer Group Structure (Z1 Closed)}]
\textbf{Status update (v17.3).} What was registered in v17.1 as
CONJ-NZ01 (a well-posed conjecture) has been promoted to a proven
theorem in the standalone paper \texttt{CRF\_Z1\_Paper\_v1\_1.tex}.
The Z1 question --- ``Is $|\mathbb{Z}_{15}| = T_5$ derivable from K35
and the matter split?'' --- is now \textbf{Closed} in the
Z-programme.

\smallskip
Under T-canonical:
\[
  \boxed{|\mathbb{Z}_{15}| \;=\; T_5 \;=\; 15
         \;=\; n_m(n_m+1)/2}
\]
where $n_m = 5$ is the integer matter-mode count, $T_5$ is the 5th
triangular number, and $\mathbb{Z}_{15}$ arises by the Chinese
Remainder Theorem from the integer factorisation
$\mathbb{Z}_{15} \cong \mathbb{Z}_3 \times \mathbb{Z}_5$
($\gcd(3,5) = 1$). The $\mathbb{Z}_3$ factor is the spatial symmetry
group from the $d_s = 3$ singleton modes; the $\mathbb{Z}_5$ factor
is the matter symmetry group from the $n_m = 5$ non-singleton modes.

\textbf{Theorem (THM-Z101)}. The proof proceeds in 4 steps via
Lemmas LEM-Z101 (Matter Split), LEM-Z102 (Key Identity
$3d_s = 2^{d_s} + 1$), and LEM-Z103 (Equivalence). Uniqueness at
$d_s = 3$ is established by COR-Z101 (failure at $d_s = 2, 4$ via
$\gcd > 1$).

\textbf{T-canonical necessity.} Under E-canonical ($d_s = 3.031$),
$\mathbb{Z}_{3.031}$ is not a group --- the conjecture would have to
be reformulated as approximate-$\mathbb{Z}_3$ in some limit. Under
T-canonical, $\mathbb{Z}_3 \times \mathbb{Z}_5 \cong \mathbb{Z}_{15}$
is algebraically clean (DEF-Z101 of the standalone paper).

\textbf{Source.} The full proof, the 16 atomic units (DEF-Z101..05,
LEM-Z101..03, THM-Z101, COR-Z101..02, EQ-Z101..06), and the verification
trace (Checks~18 and 19 of the v3 suite, both gap $0.0000\%$) are in
\texttt{CRF\_Z1\_Paper\_v1\_1.tex} per the standalone-paper-first
protocol (CLM-AM04). This section condenses the integrated narrative.
\end{formalbox}

\begin{claim}[CLM-NZ03: T-Canonical is Required for the Symmetry Programme]
The Noether $\mathbb{Z}_{15}$ programme (questions Z1--Z5) requires
integer $d_s$ to even be statable. Any rigorous group-theoretic CRF
derivation lives in T-gauge by structural necessity. Under T-canonical:
$|\mathbb{Z}_{15}| = T_5 = 15$ exact (Check~18 of v3 verification,
gap $0.0000\%$); $T_8/T_5 = 36/15 = 12/5$ exact (Check~19,
gap $0.0000\%$).
\end{claim}

drift.

joins the Z-programme as a closure deliverable. Its 16 atomic units
(DEF-Z101..05, LEM-Z101..03, THM-Z101, COR-Z101..02, EQ-Z101..06)

questions (Z2, Z3), and two lower-priority items (Z4, Z5).

\smallskip

joins the Z-programme as the second closure deliverable. The

GAP-Z202 (anomaly cancellation) via THM-CB03 (ZC03a + ZC03b,
